# Volleyball Studio — full Rust rewrite ledger

**Status:** ACTIVE — full Rust rewrite; acceptance and performance comparison happen after implementation.
**As of 2026-11-09:** the active migration branch is Rust-only. The original Python source now lives only in protected Git history/checkpoint, not as a parallel runnable app. Rust is the sole entrypoint but remaining product features are not yet complete. No network/GPU/real-match readiness is implied.
**Decision:** 2026-11-07. **Branch:** `migration/rust-2026-11-07`.
**Handoff:** `docs/RUST_MIGRATION.md`. **Coverage:** `docs/RUST_REWRITE_SCOPE.md`.

> **User-approved replacement of the old strategy:** First rewrite the **entire application** in Rust, then test and adapt the integrated Rust version, and **only after that** compare it with the Python original. If the outcome does not justify migration, check out the protected Python checkpoint. S00/S01/S02a tests below are historical results, NOT progressive gates or justification for a hybrid rollout.

## 1. Target architecture and safety contract

1. **Complete Rust application**: project/profile/history management, video preparation, GPU inference, CPU processing, tracking/interpolation, Touch/rallies, ReID/players, photos/statistics, preview, video editing/montage/quick reel, API, jobs, storage and packaging. No Python server, CPU backend, or Python orchestration in the finished product.
2. **Browser UI included**: migrate application behavior from `scripts/volleyball_ui/static/` to Rust/WebAssembly. HTML, CSS and minimal browser bootstrap can remain as platform assets. Interim JavaScript during development is NOT a complete rewrite.
3. **No unnecessary rewrite of vendor runtimes**: CUDA/model providers (ONNX Runtime/OpenVINO) and FFmpeg remain third-party native binaries/libraries invoked from Rust. This does not count as retaining Python business logic.
4. **Preserve semantics**: project -> versioned cached GPU evidence -> versioned CPU interpretation -> players/rallies/stats -> original-quality montage. Preserve existing run formats, saved user projects, raw detections/IDs, manual priorities, golden configuration and RU/EN UI behavior.
5. **No premature cutover**: original Python application and `master` remain intact. Implement Rust independently; run it on a separate development port with separate disposable outputs when necessary. Do not run duplicate servers on 8765, stop live jobs, delete user media, or mutate old project data.
6. **Comparison after full implementation only**: earlier isolated parity or microbenchmarks are historical diagnostics. Measure quality and speed end-to-end on identical real videos and hardware only after the Rust product is fully implemented and functionally adapted.

References: `docs/ARCHITECTURE_INFERENCE_POSTPROCESSING.md`, `docs/UI_LAYOUT_POLICY.md`, `docs/DEVELOPMENT_WORKFLOW.md`, `docs/TESTING.md`.

## 2. Complete rewrite plan — replaces incremental test gates

Progress can be committed in coherent batches and compilation checked, but **no stage-by-stage approval, parity-gating or default switching**. First implement all workstreams, then integrated testing/adaptation, then compare against the original.

| Workstream | Original sources | New Rust ownership | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| R00 — checkpoint and coverage | `scripts/`, `src/`, `ov/` | `docs/RUST_REWRITE_SCOPE.md` | BASELINE SAVED |
| R01 — API/job/process management | `scripts/volleyball_ui/server.py`, service modules | `rust/volley-app/` | PARTIAL: native JSON API, project/montage/Quick Reel preparation jobs, MP4 byte-range responses and preview; GPU inference and complete CPU/Quick Reel flow pending |
| R02 — projects, profiles, caches | `scripts/volleyball_ui/projects.py`, `analysis_runs.py`, `prepared_videos.py` | `rust/volley-app/` | PARTIAL: projects, profiles, runs/history and autoqueued isolated prepared-video cache; cleanup pending |
| R03 — video preparation and model inference GPU | `src/inference*.py`, `scripts/volleyball_studio/analysis*.py` | `rust/volley-inference/`, `rust/volley-media/` | PARTIAL: FFmpeg prep/cache plus native ORT VballNet/RAVEL 9-frame session kernel and read-only diagnostics; full streaming GPU decoding, player tracking, CPU handoff and real-device benchmark pending |
| R04 — trajectories/tracks/Touch/CPU | `src/player_contacts.py`, `scripts/volleyball_studio/reprocess.py` | `rust/volley-core/`, `rust/volley-app/` | PARTIAL: IoU/motion, native RAVEL peak NMS, bounded raw RAVEL cache reader, stable Track ID association and motion/reach diagnostic; full CPU replay and extrema processing pending |
| R05 — ReID, players, rallies and statistics | `src/player_reid.py`, player services | `rust/volley-core/`, `rust/volley-app/` | PARTIAL: native continuity and ReID, isolated manual links and candidate groups, CPU action deduplication and versioned per-player statistics; image feature extraction, full native CPU replay, global roster editor and UI pending |
| R06 — preview, montage, audio, quick reel | `src/make_reels.py`, `scripts/volleyball_ui/*montage*.py`, `quick_reel*.py` | `rust/volley-media/`, `rust/volley-app/` | PARTIAL: original-source montage, verified-player action filters, versioned gold bookmarks, soundtrack; standalone Quick Reel source selection and cancellable preparation/status now available, but new GPU/CPU inference/render/export and UI remain pending |
| R07 — complete UI and localization | `scripts/volleyball_ui/static/` | `rust/volley-web/` (Rust/WASM and assets) | NOT STARTED |
| R08 — packaging, launch and deployment | `start-volley-ui.cmd`, `Dockerfile` | standalone Rust launch/deployment | NOT STARTED |
| R09 — **after R01–R08** full tests and adaptation | All E2E workflows | integrated Rust application | DEFERRED |
| R10 — **after R09** comparison and choice | Frozen original Python vs Rust | quality/perf report and revision choice | DEFERRED |

Detailed functional inventory, required file formats and parity targets: `docs/RUST_REWRITE_SCOPE.md`. A stub, Python bridge or unimplemented HTTP route is not “complete.” Progress commits must state missing functionality. Independent compilation or smoke checks are debugging aids, not final acceptance.

## 3. Final evaluation and rollback protocol

1. Finish the Rust application end to end, including GPU inference, original-quality video export and every existing UI workflow, without Python backend dependence.
2. **Only then** run comprehensive API, CPU/GPU, UI, projects, ReID, touch, rally, statistics, cancellation, cache, rendering and backward-compatibility tests. Adapt and fix the complete Rust version.
3. **Then** compare both on exactly the same videos, model versions, machine/GPU, saved observations and equivalent settings. Evaluate speed, RAM/VRAM, touch precision/recall, player ID accuracy, rally boundaries, video quality and UI responsiveness. Record real measurements rather than assuming Rust wins.
4. Switch production only when the final result is worthwhile. Otherwise restore `checkpoint/pre-rust-2026-11-07` at `aeaf7704ac4228affe04f8e50fbc52769c44b725`, or another explicitly selected known-good Python commit. Preserve all local projects, media and caches and retain the Rust development branch for future work; never force-reset `master`.
5. Draft PR #7 stays unmerged until this final decision. No paid hosted CI assumption; local testing after completion is documented.

**Historical record below is retained unchanged.** Earlier “next gate” comments in S01/S02a are superseded by this decision.

### S01 Rust kernel and parity — 2026-11-07

**Status:** S01 local acceptance PASSED and GitHub-published commit `2d251b0c86e53fdd0ee62b4b5133cac06585d3c3` (same Git tree as local `ed5bb80114efa09e830619e3374ba232f45e52d7`). No default behavior change.



- New crate `rust/volley-core` (Rust 1.99.1; versioned ABI 1, `cdylib` plus `rlib`), implemented `bbox_iou` with the exact epsilon/degenerate-box semantics of CPU replay.

- Added `scripts/volleyball_studio/rust_core.py` ctypes adapter, `tests/test_rust_core_contract.py` parity tests and optional call site in `scripts/volleyball_studio/replay_player_tracking.py`. Default `VOLLEY_RUST_IOU` unset uses Python. Set to `1` only after building. Missing DLL or incorrect ABI fails explicitly; never silently substitutes an algorithm.

- Build: `cargo test --manifest-path rust/volley-core/Cargo.toml` — PASS (4 Rust tests); `cargo build --manifest-path rust/volley-core/Cargo.toml` — PASS; `cargo fmt --manifest-path rust/volley-core/Cargo.toml -- --check` — PASS.

- `VOLLEY_RUST_REQUIRED=1 python -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_rust_core_contract.py` — PASS (3 Python tests, including 2,506 fixed and seeded random bbox pairs across compiled Rust and original Python).

- `python -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_cpu_player_identities.py` — PASS (3); `python -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_ci_quality.py` — PASS (11); `python -m py_compile ...` and `git diff --check` — PASS.

- Windows 32-bit Python 3.14 cannot load x86_64 Rust DLL: separately tested explicit diagnostic `Rust volley-core DLL requires 64-bit Python on Windows` — PASS. Parity was tested with `python` 3.11.9 **64-bit**. Keep 32-bit Python on Python fallback or install Python 3.14 x64, do not misrepresent this as production Python 3.14 testing.

- Microbenchmark (same Windows host, 80,000 scalar calls, seeded inputs): Python IoU **0.0477 s**, cached Rust ctypes IoU **0.1264 s**, identical checksum **270.905997457**. An earlier uncached adapter took **15.3821 s** due to repeated file checks; caching fixed it. Scalar FFI is still slower than pure Python for this tiny operation, so do **not enable Rust by default**. Future stages should move whole loops/batches across the boundary and benchmark end-to-end CPU replay before opting in.

- `tests/test_player_three_second_gap.py` — **NOT TESTED** on this Python 3.11 environment: import fails because `cv2` is missing. No runtime library installs, GPU jobs, media, or user caches were modified to force this test.

- Full E2E project replay, annotated real-match accuracy, Docker and GPU — **NOT TESTED**. S01 implements only deterministic geometry/parity, not an overall faster tracker.

- Rollback: leave `VOLLEY_RUST_IOU` unset; if necessary restore the entire branch to S00 remote `8ebca9f64f5a4fe57999e0c3af01880a34d13075` (retain user data and checkpoint).





### S02a Rust batched motion validation — 2026-11-07

**Status:** Local deterministic-parity gate PASSED; S02a GitHub commit `4ccb60bbf907a586cfb8d6d88b1c0c4d2156e412` (same tree as local `0e45b46e97c24289e52e9391f0c66664c1fe3a46`). S02 overall remains INCOMPLETE pending annotated-match evaluation and other touch-peak logic.



- Scope: move the gravity-corrected velocity fits and motion-break selection for RAVEL touch evidence to `rust/volley-core/src/motion.rs`. Safe C ABI `volley_ball_motion_batch_v1` consumes saved ball observations and all target peak frames in one call. No model, raw cache, golden thresholds, identity assignment or montage data is changed.

- The existing `scripts/volleyball_studio/reprocess.py` preserves the Python path unless the explicit environment flag `VOLLEY_RUST_MOTION=1` is set. `_ravel_contacts` batches proposed peak frames for one Rust call rather than copying the ball track for each candidate.

- Rust uses parameter values from `src/player_contacts.py` passed by Python in `scripts/volleyball_studio/rust_core.py`, avoiding diverging numerical defaults. The output schema, rounding, frame selection, rejection of gravity-only flight and touch gates were preserved.

- `cargo test --manifest-path rust/volley-core/Cargo.toml` — PASS (7 Rust tests, including the 4 S01 tests). `cargo build` — PASS. `cargo fmt --manifest-path rust/volley-core/Cargo.toml` applied formatting and `cargo fmt -- --check` is required before commit.

- `VOLLEY_RUST_REQUIRED=1 python -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_rust_motion_contract.py` — PASS (4 Python tests, 90 seeded noisy trajectories plus straight/gravity/reversal/speed-gain/sparse tracks, default-vs-Rust identical dictionaries, and malformed input diagnostics).

- Existing `python -m unittest discover -s tests -p test_ravel_touch_motion.py` — PASS (7 tests); with `VOLLEY_RUST_MOTION=1` — PASS (7 tests). Identical input touch results in both modes.

- Synthetic microbenchmark on same HOME-PC: 500 saved trajectory observations, 96 candidate frames per run, 25 runs; Python **0.1227 s**, Rust batch **0.0179 s** (~6.9x faster on this microbenchmark), with exact matching evidence. This is **NOT** a measured full-video / production speedup.

- Real annotated-match acceptance, GPU inference, CPU full quality suite, 64-bit Python 3.14 environment and Docker — **NOT TESTED**. Do not change golden defaults or automatically enable Rust. Retain original Python path for rollback by simply unsetting `VOLLEY_RUST_MOTION`.

- Next gate S02b: port the remaining Touch candidate/peak or CPU geometry work only after repeatable baseline fixtures are available, and compare actual Touch precision/recall and event timing on the same cached inference. Do not equate synthetic equality with real-match quality.



## 5. Handoff to the next AI / engineer

1. Inspect branch, tracked status, `docs/RUST_REWRITE_SCOPE.md`, and this ledger. Work in `migration/rust-2026-11-07`, do not discard untracked media/backup files.
2. **Implement the entire Rust application before the formal test/adaptation/comparison phase.** No intermediate opt-in rollout, Python fallback as the target architecture, or blocked progression due to per-kernel benchmark.
3. Preserve public API contracts, old files/project history, all GPU inference and CPU features, 2+ language UI, cached media, cancellation, player identity workflow, montage, quick reel, presets and settings.
4. Rust-native application owns orchestration. FFmpeg and model runtime can be external. Frontend application logic must become Rust/WASM, not remain the previous JavaScript permanently.
5. Checkpoints are allowed: commit and publish coherent incomplete work, mark gaps accurately in `docs/RUST_REWRITE_SCOPE.md`; compilation checks are okay. Do not call placeholders complete. Do not touch the production `master` or existing job.
6. Once ALL parts are implemented, comprehensively test/adapt; only then compare real project outcomes to the Python checkpoint and decide cutover/rollback. No fabricated metrics.
7. Publish via GitHub connector if local `git push` still has no credentials. Draft PR: https://github.com/Amazomic/fast-volleyball-tracking-inference/pull/7.

## 6. Superseding decision — 2026-11-07

- User explicitly required full rewrite first, tests/adaptation second, comparison third and Git rollback if unsatisfactory.
- S00, S01 and S02a work survives as reusable code; their opt-in hybrid is not the desired final product.
- Existing running hybrid service is not proof of full Rust completion. No production cutover is authorized.
- This decision changes development order only, not data safety or acceptance standards.

## 7. R01 initial implementation checkpoint — 2026-11-07

**Status: PARTIAL (NOT A COMPLETE RUST APPLICATION).** Started an independent Rust backend at `rust/volley-app/` using Axum/Tokio, with explicit incomplete-build status and a separate loopback-only development port (8875). Its `GET /api/projects` reads `results/projects/projects.json` natively and preserves existing project records without invoking Python or writing user data. `GET /internal/rewrite-status` returns `migration_complete=false` and `production_ready=false`. The app deliberately does **not** expose the normal `/api/health` endpoint, so it cannot masquerade as production API v28. Exclusive port binding rejects duplicate listeners.

- Linux WSL cargo toolchain available and used to compile dependencies; `cargo check --manifest-path rust/volley-app/Cargo.toml` **PASSED**, with one unused-import warning corrected afterward.
- All remaining server endpoints, persistent jobs, GPU inference, full postprocessing, montage and Rust/WASM UI: **NOT IMPLEMENTED**. Full tests, real-video metrics and quality comparison: **DEFERRED** until full rewrite, per user decision.
- The existing service on `127.0.0.1:8765`, old Python checkpoint and saved projects were not modified by this checkpoint. The new Rust server has not replaced or taken over production.

## 8. Full rewrite continuation: native project/catalog/history/media layers — 2026-11-07

**Implementation status: PARTIAL; application not ready to replace Python.**

- Consolidated new Rust code into a three-crate workspace at `rust/Cargo.toml` with **one** `rust/Cargo.lock`: `volley-core` (historical Rust kernels), `volley-app` (new Axum/Tokio API) and `volley-media` (native FFprobe/media metadata).
- `rust/volley-app/src/projects.rs`: project registry summaries in the actual browser `{"projects":[...]}` shape, source video availability/cached-analysis pointers, read-only source video catalog, and native create/update of project registries. Experimental writes require `VOLLEY_RUST_DATA_ROOT` pointing at an **isolated absolute directory** outside the original `results/`, and origin-checked POST. Registry writes use a dedicated file lock and atomic JSON files. This does **not yet queue video preparation**.
- `rust/volley-app/src/catalog.rs`: native global profile listing, athlete library listing, source video listing, resource estimates and `GET /api/video-info`.
- `rust/volley-app/src/history.rs`: native analysis/experiment timing history built from saved complete runs and valid stage records.
- `rust/volley-app/src/analysis_runs.rs`: native GPU run and CPU snapshot/version enumeration, run renames/hidden IDs, cache/source ownership validation and deterministic legacy CPU-setting hashes. Does **not** alter saved project files or materialize branches.
- `rust/volley-app/src/storage.rs`: JSON handling, no-traversal identifiers, origin checks, isolated data root guard and atomic writes.
- `rust/volley-media/src/lib.rs`: FFprobe metadata (rotation, display/coded size, duration, auto analysis width), exact H.264/YUV420P/30 FPS/AAC/no-rotation readiness filter for skipping unnecessary transcodes. External FFmpeg/FFprobe remain native tools; **not Python**.
- `rust/volley-app/src/main.rs`: dev-only loopback listener on port `8875` (configurable), no fake production `/api/health`, route list and accurate `migration_complete=false` flag. Existing Studio on port `8765` remains untouched.
- Verified after code changes in Ubuntu-25.04: `cargo fmt --all --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml -- --check`, `cargo check --workspace --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml` and `cargo build --workspace --manifest-path rust/Cargo.toml` — **PASS**. Full functional/API/browser tests and behavior validation are **DEFERRED** until the full rewrite, per user instruction.
- **Not attempted by design:** full Python-vs-Rust parity, real annotated match comparison, GPU inference, FFmpeg renders, all-app E2E and production cutover. These occur after implementation of all workstreams under the owner decision. Native Rust registry creation is isolated from the existing user data until that stage.
- Outstanding compatibility concerns for the later adaptation phase: legacy imported player identities and source aliases, project preparation queue on create/update, source metadata refresh semantics, CPU run manifest variations, and full browser API coverage.

## 9. Native Rust video-preparation lifecycle — 2026-11-07

**Status: PARTIAL IMPLEMENTATION; NOT the complete Rust application.** This work continues R01–R03 under the user-directed **finish whole Rust rewrite first → integrate/test/adapt → compare Python** sequence.

- `rust/volley-media/src/preparation.rs`: new content-addressed video-preparation plan, schema-2 fingerprint (canonical source path, byte size, nanosecond modification time, requested width, FPS 30) and Python-compatible sorted/ASCII-escaped SHA256 cache identifier. Detect already analysis-ready MP4 and skip transcode entirely. Cached prepared MP4 is reused only when the saved identity and ready status match and the file is nonempty.
- `rust/volley-app/src/preparation.rs`: Rust/Tokio one-at-a-time FFmpeg queue and per-project subscribers; shared source identity deduplication across projects; exclusive prepared-output file lock across Rust processes; unique partial filenames; FFmpeg progress parsing; atomic ready manifest publication; cancellation and cleanup of spawned child process; errors persisted to isolated dev status files. Input videos remain read-only.
- The preparation status endpoint returns the established `project_id/videos/ready/total` response structure, including per-video `state/progress/width/fps/error/size_mb/preparation`. Explicit cancel persists a per-project pause marker until a deliberate retry so refresh/polling cannot restart cancelled work.
- New/updated Rust sandbox projects enqueue preparation automatically; if background scheduling fails after the project record was durably committed, do not turn it into a create failure that would produce duplicate projects on retry.
- Development writes require an absolute `VOLLEY_RUST_DATA_ROOT` outside **the entire original repository**, not merely outside `results/`. The root is resolved and checked before directory creation. Existing Python UI and data on 8765 remain untouched. The default read-only Rust server cannot launch preparation.
- Routes: `GET /api/project-preparation?project_id=...`, `POST /api/project-preparation/retry`, `POST /api/project-preparation/cancel`. These are still experimental Rust routes, not a claim of all-API completeness.
- Rust modules use native FFprobe/FFmpeg commands, **not Python**. Cached source metadata/probe plans are reused while source size/mtime remain identical; no expensive GPU inference occurs during video normalization.
- **Development diagnostics only:** `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo check --workspace` and `cargo build --workspace` in WSL Ubuntu completed; formal functional, UI, GPU, cancellation under real FFmpeg load, cross-project historical-cache adoption and Python-vs-Rust comparisons remain **DEFERRED until whole rewrite** by the user's request.
- Remaining: model inference, tracking, ReID, GPU/CPU job orchestration, old-project prepared-cache promotion, media streaming, preview/UI, montage/reels, packaging and full end-to-end verification.

## 10. Rust-native HTTP preview and prepared MP4 streaming — 2026-11-07

**Status: PARTIAL — independent Rust handlers exist; whole application remains INCOMPLETE.**

- `rust/volley-media/src/preview.rs`: Rust-native frame request validation (source/analysis mode, selected width, 30 FPS frame index), ffmpeg image-to-pipe invocation with a 60-second timeout and bounded JPEG output. Original source video is never overwritten.
- `rust/volley-app/src/playback.rs`: `GET /api/video-preview` returns JPEG directly and reads/writes a content-addressed preview cache. With the default read-only Rust mode it only reads preexisting cache and generates misses in memory; experimental writes go strictly to the isolated Rust data root.
- `GET /api/video-view` reports prepared-media availability and player-compatible playback fields; `GET /api/video-media` streams the validated prepared source or cached MP4 from Rust/Tokio with `Accept-Ranges`, `Content-Range`, single-range HTTP 206 and unsatisfiable-range HTTP 416. Player/project membership is checked before serving media; unprepared videos are not silently replaced by the raw original.
- Concurrent FFmpeg preview decodes are bounded by a two-request semaphore. Frame caches derive from original name, source size and nanosecond mtime; media requests never invoke Python.
- Added direct dependencies on tokio-util and percent-encoding for bounded asynchronous stream I/O and safe URL construction; Rust media crate uses Tokio for FFmpeg timeout/process management.
- Development compiler checks are permitted; integrated functional/API/browser tests and annotated Python-vs-Rust evaluation remain **DEFERRED until complete Rust rewrite**, as expressly requested.
- **Not implemented:** original-project legacy prepared-cache migration, fallback segment encoding, player/rally overlays, other preview analysis endpoints, UI/WebAssembly, GPU models, final montage/reel pipeline, comprehensive API compatibility.

## 11. Native-source manual montage engine and sandbox API — 2026-11-07

**Status: PARTIAL implementation only.** This is another part of the complete rewrite; **do not switch the working Python application or declare montage functionality complete**.

- `rust/volley-media/src/montage.rs`: Rust types for manually selected clips and original-recording offsets; exact source seek is `analysis_offset_sec + start_frame / 30`, segment lengths are inclusive end frames and use the native FFprobe nominal frame rate. No use of resized inference/video cache as render input. H.264 high-quality libx264 CRF15/fast CPU path, optional NVENC CQ17/p5 path and fallback. Single clip/concat demuxer stream-copy when all segments are compatible; mixed formats use an FFmpeg concat filter, preserve the largest native source pixel dimensions and pad differing aspect ratios. Optional looped/faded soundtrack audio is muxed without re-encoding the finished video stream.
- `rust/volley-app/src/montage.rs`: bounded manual selected-clips request (80 scenes, up to 600 seconds); project/video membership verification, bounded seek and audio file validation, isolated Rust-only output path under `projects/<id>/montages/<job>/`, per-job `selection.json`, single job slot, progress based on FFmpeg pipe, GPU-to-CPU fallback without backwards progress, explicit cancellation/child termination, safe error tail buffering, durable versioned outputs, saved montage library and byte-range MP4 media access.
- New experimental endpoints: `POST /api/montage/native/start`, `GET /api/montage/native/status`, `POST /api/montage/native/cancel`, `GET /api/montage/native/library`, `GET /api/montage/native/media`, `POST /api/montage/native/audio` for bounded multipart music upload. Uploaded names are generated identifiers, never raw client-supplied paths. Sandbox read/write protection remains in effect. `/api/highlights` and the legacy UI contract have **not** been replaced yet.
- Important remaining work for this pipeline: derive manual scene boundaries/rally IDs and offsets from saved CPU/inference evidence rather than trusting caller-supplied frame ranges/offsets, implement automated score/action/marked filters, track/ball follow framing, preserve user-selected rally sequences, old montage/quick-reel APIs and browser controls, add project-level cancellation/deletion integration and proper handling of cross-run exports.
- **Verified for development:** `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo check --workspace` and `cargo build --workspace` on WSL Ubuntu-25.04 completed without warnings or errors. **Not yet run:** real-video montage generation with the new endpoints, GPU encoder fallback under real load, final rendered quality measurements, UI regression, GPU inference, full automated end-to-end functional validation, or Python-vs-Rust comparison. These remain deferred until full implementation per user instruction.
- The working Studio on `127.0.0.1:8765`, original Git `master`, checkpoint, project videos and analysis caches are unchanged by this migration work.

## 12. Native CPU-evidence rally reconstruction and selection — 2026-11-07

**Status: PARTIAL. Not a final replacement for Python rally/UI processing.** This stage implements the selection core before the global E2E test phase requested by the owner.

- `rust/volley-core/src/rallies.rs`: standalone Rust rally grouping from classified ball-track intervals, serve/contact events and saved ranking observations. Neighbouring rally fragments can merge within 3 seconds by default, except across a confirmed technical-return segment or a reliable serve event. Context padding respects nearby rallies and next serves, without claiming the last ball track is proof of point end. Ranking and action counts are derived from saved observations, with per-track feature fallback for old runs. Selection uses intact interval frames; duration budgets do not trim an individual rally. Stable `rally_id` is a SHA256 hash of Python-compatible serialized source video/cache/bounds/track IDs. This Rust core runs without a Python subprocess.
- `rust/volley-app/src/rallies.rs`: read-only API `POST /api/montage/native/plan`. Mandatory `project_id` and `run_id`; resolve only a run visible in the project's Rust run registry (including CPU snapshots), validate per-video cache manifest/source, and read `analysis/game-tracks.json`, `events.json` and optionally `ranking.json`. Automatic mode requires ranking, applies selectable strategy, minimum detected duration/confidence, per-action thresholds and a strict total-time cap. Manual mode admits only `selected_rally_ids` from the same selected cache; its candidates include unranked intervals. Original project video order is preserved. No user-supplied frame/offset is trusted by this route.
- `rust/volley-app/src/montage.rs`: new experimental `POST /api/montage/native/start-from-run` endpoint. It derives clips from the server-selected CPU-run candidates and passes verified frame bounds and analysis offsets into the native-source Rust FFmpeg montage engine. Rendering remains restricted to an isolated writable Rust dev root. The legacy `/api/montage/native/start` still exists for raw manual ranges; it is **not** a validated rally-selection endpoint.
- Updated `rust/volley-app/src/main.rs`, the Rust crate dependency graph and API inventory. These routes are not wired to the production browser and do not switch the app on port `8765`.
- **Remaining**: complete postprocessing Touch/retracking, ReID/player identity, player statistics, persist/reload gold-marked rally filtering, UI rally and montage workflows, event parity and accuracy adaptation, inference/quick reel/render follow-ball/vertical overlays. The saved event classification is consumed, not recomputed by this stage.
- The current build only verifies compilation/formatting; comprehensive model/video tests and accuracy comparison remain deferred until the complete Rust rewrite as requested.

## 13. Rust-only RAVEL Touch peak and motion diagnostic — 2026-11-07

**Status: PARTIAL CPU implementation; not a full project CPU replay.** Migration remains intentionally unmerged into production until all modules are rewritten and evaluated.

- `rust/volley-core/src/touch.rs`: native Touch candidate selection with strongest per-frame RAVEL probability, NumPy-like float32 centered smoothing, stable strongest-first non-maximum suppression, actor assignment near a shifted temporal peak, then verified tracking-ID association by non-interpolated bounding-box IoU >= 0.3 within ±2 frames. Unknown IDs remain unknown; raw model IDs are never treated as saved stable player identities.
- A confirmed RAVEL contact additionally requires a reachable ball observation within ±2 frames and a gravitationally corrected discontinuity in velocity/direction, using the existing native `rust/volley-core/src/motion.rs` computation. A ball flying straight through a player rectangle alone is not a touch. Nearby positives are collapsed into one event. Output `unknown_touch` does not invent a play technique.
- `rust/volley-app/src/touch_preview.rs`: new read-only `POST /api/cpu/touch-preview` for an explicit `project_id`, `run_id`, `video` and optional `threshold`, `smooth`, `min_gap`, `max_reach_ratio`. Resolves the chosen run through saved project registry, validates the immutable inference ownership and RAVEL-013 manifest, streams the saved raw JSONL as bounded frame candidates, associates stable IDs from saved `players.json` or CPU `players.replay.json`, and checks the selected CPU version's classified `game-tracks.json` observations when present. Reuses the saved neural predictions without invoking Python or GPU.
- Diagnostic reports include raw proposal counts, candidate peaks, verified Track IDs, motion-supported contacts and an explicit `motion_evidence_available` flag. If a GPU-only run has no CPU tracks, only candidate peaks are returned; the diagnostic does not fabricate motion-validated contacts. Request concurrency is bounded to one run per development server.
- This endpoint is not persisted back to the original project and does not alter `analysis/events.json`, personal player stats or montage results. No full threshold-dependent postprocessing snapshot is published yet. Fine-grained geometric technique classification, ball extrema, native player tracking, ReID and whole CPU pipeline orchestration remain outstanding.
- Source Python behavior inspected: `scripts/volleyball_studio/touch_peaks.py`, `scripts/volleyball_studio/reprocess.py`, `src/player_contacts.py`. As agreed, development `cargo fmt/check/build` verifies compilation only; real-match accuracy/performance and full E2E parity tests remain deferred until the entire Rust rewrite.

## 14. Native provisional player continuity and safe Touch grouping — 2026-11-07

**PARTIAL implementation.** The target remains a standalone Rust Studio; no migration to the active UI or real player auto-enrollment is authorized by this work.

- `rust/volley-core/src/continuity.rs`: Rust-only geometry tracklet grouping ported from `scripts/volleyball_studio/player_continuity.py`. Extracts first/last frame, normalized box center/height and observation count from non-interpolated, score >=0.3 player detections. Short links require at least 5 observations per Track ID, a <=20-frame gap at 30 FPS, height ratio <=1.18, center distance <=0.040, score >=0.75 and **reciprocally unique** best candidate with a 0.12 margin over competing candidates. Linked tracks cannot temporally overlap. Neither linked candidate groups nor their scores are confirmed real-world person identities.
- Optional extended-gap suggestions last up to **4 seconds**. This is a **different, stricter, read-only suggestion path**: at least 10 observations per track, height ratio <=1.09, normalized center distance <=0.018, score >=0.82, reciprocal uniqueness, plus endpoints at the terminal/head of existing short-gap groups. Long-gap suggestions **never merge Track IDs**, cannot overwrite manual assignments, and do not create player IDs. A four-second track absence does not prove the returning person is the same.
- `rust/volley-app/src/player_continuity.rs`: `POST /api/players/continuity-preview` reads one explicitly selected `project_id`, `run_id` and `video` after verifying project/run/cache ownership. Uses saved `players.replay.json` when available, or the original `players.json`. Reads inference-scoped manually excluded IDs from `results/projects/<project>/player_identities.json` without any write or migration side effect. Response includes schema-1 compatible `proposed_links`, `groups`, `track_to_candidate`, plus separate `extended_suggestions`. Input-size limits and one-at-a-time requests protect memory/desktop responsiveness.
- `rust/volley-app/src/touch_preview.rs`: native read-only Touch diagnostic now checks the saved exclusions before associating a Touch peak to a stored Track ID. For motion-confirmed touches it returns `unconfirmed_candidate_touch_counts` and `touches_without_candidate_id`, keeping the direct stable Track ID unchanged. **These candidate-group counts are not player statistics and must not be used to auto-assign identities.**
- Reused the Rust motion validation from the previous stage. No additional Python subprocess or GPU inference. Primary Python service, original project media, result snapshots and immutable checkpoint remain untouched.
- Still missing: rebuilding original detector IDs with proper 3–4s temporal hysteresis, appearance-based ReID, saved/photo reference matching, manually confirmed group-to-person linking, player touch/statistics snapshots, complete CPU pipeline and UI controls. Compilation and formatting checks are development diagnostics; final real-match testing/adaptation/parity comparison happen only after complete rewrite, per owner instruction.

## 15. Rust-native cached ReID comparison — 2026-11-07

**PARTIAL. Read-only evaluation, not yet a native image embedding encoder or manual identity editor.**

- rust/volley-core/src/reid.rs: native L2-normalized cosine matrix of every available Track ID, top-three nearby compatible matches, threshold 55–99, complete-link clustering without indirect bridges. Co-visible players, conflicting reviewed owners, and ambiguous competing identities cannot be silently merged. Very short tracks need stronger evidence; color fallback embeddings require stronger cosine and close temporal/spatial endpoints. Cosine percentages are not calibrated identity probabilities.
- rust/volley-app/src/reid_npz.rs: bounded ZIP/NumPy archive reader, with float16/32/64 vectors, Unicode ID lists and model fingerprints. Rejects object/pickle arrays, corrupt dimensions and oversized archives. Reuses existing original cached embedding matrices, without running Python or GPU.
- rust/volley-app/src/reid_native.rs: new POST /api/players/reid-native/preview requires a known project, run and video, verifies cache source/model fingerprint, honors excluded IDs, manual splits, manual groups and reviewed player assignments. Reads vetted player album embedding caches for same-model photo reference suggestions only when album index/model signatures match. Self-references are suppressed. Results contain groups, nearest tracks, ranked possible owners, reference counts, and auto_assign_enabled:false. No writes to original inference or identity files.
- Remaining: native OpenVINO photo embedding extraction and new NPZ publication, photo album editing, persisted manual identity links and player-statistics updates, full ReID workflow UI, GPU and CPU pipelines, quality/performance acceptance. No E2E comparisons until migration is complete under the user's plan.

## 16. Isolated Rust manual player identities — 2026-11-07

**PARTIAL**: manual approval/rejection persistence is now implemented in Rust; the Python UI, inference outputs and original project metadata remain unchanged until final feature parity and validation.

- File: `rust/volley-app/src/player_identity.rs`. A single experimental `POST /api/players/identity/native` route supports `read`, `assign`, `unassign`, `exclude`, and `restore`. It resolves a specifically named project/run/video through the saved analysis registry and checks each edited Track ID against immutable GPU `players.json` in that inference. Assignments use the stable `video::base_gpu_cache` key, so CPU snapshots of one GPU inference share manually approved identities.
- Only players already in the selected project roster and global player catalog may receive assignments. Whole saved ReID groups can be assigned using `group_id` only after verifying the group archive's source-file metadata and known member IDs. Max 200 IDs/operation; duplicate or foreign Track IDs are rejected.
- Manual assignment writes `link_meta[track_id] = { source: manual, reviewed: true }`. Moving an ID between distinct already assigned owners requires explicit `allow_reassign:true`. `unassign` records an auto-rejection so stale ReID proposals cannot immediately restore the link. `exclude` removes links, records both exclusion and auto-rejection; `restore` makes the detection visible again while keeping auto-rejection and never detaching other existing owner assignments.
- Concurrency: read returns an immutable SHA256 `revision` of the identity file (or `absent`). Every mutation requires matching `expected_revision`, checked again *inside an exclusive file lock*; saves are atomic (temporary JSON + rename). A bounded eight-source cache of immutable GPU Track ID lists prevents reparsing large `players.json` files on every button click and invalidates by source path, mtime and size.
- Safety: modification is blocked unless `VOLLEY_RUST_DATA_ROOT` points to an isolated writable root outside the production repository. Project paths, source caches, target symlinks, group data, file sizes and player IDs are validated; no photo gallery or original GPU bytes are mutated. Writes are scoped to `<isolated-root>/projects/<project_id>/player_identities.json`; read-only preview remains available without the writable root.
- **Pending**: editing manual ReID candidate groups (merge/split) and persistent group catalogue generation, native photo capture/reference enrollment, global player create/edit and roster assignment, canonical aggregation of events/touches into per-player stats and preview overlays, full Rust/WASM UI. This is not a production switch.

## 17. Native manual ReID candidate group merge/split — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL.** The ReID candidate editor is independent of confirmed athlete identity and cannot auto-link Track IDs to real players.

- `rust/volley-app/src/reid_group_edits.rs` adds `POST /api/players/reid-native/groups` with `action: read|merge|split` scoped to an explicitly chosen `project_id`, `run_id`, and `video`. It resolves the inference-version `players.json` via Rust run registry, validates the original source's mtime/size stamp, and persists the Python-compatible schema-1 ReID group catalogue at `<isolated-root>/projects/<project_id>/player_identity_groups/<sha24>.json`.
- When a group catalogue is absent, `read` computes a **read-only** baseline from the already cached .npz model vectors using the native ReID engine. It returns revision `absent`; the first explicit merge/split initializes the catalogue atomically. If a saved catalogue belongs to a different GPU source, it fails closed instead of discarding or silently applying manual decisions.
- `split` removes selected Track IDs from candidate groups and adds them to `manual_excluded_ids`, so subsequent native ReID threshold adjustments do not merge them automatically. Remaining group members are retained and pinned as manual only if at least two remain. `merge` pins **exactly** the selected IDs, preserving unselected members; it clears their manual split suppression. Neither operation creates a confirmed player assignment.
- Merge guardrails: only existing Track IDs from the chosen inference, max 200 per edit; never merge simultaneously visible tracks, excluded IDs, IDs with contradictory manual assignment, or Track IDs confirmed as different players. Manual edits preserve candidate-group nonoverlap. Before writing, it checks both the group SHA256 revision and the GPU source stamp, protects against symlinks, acquires the same cross-process identity lock as real player assignments plus a per-group lock, and writes through atomic JSON replacement in an isolated development root.
- `rust/volley-app/src/player_identity.rs` now requires `expected_group_revision` when assigning a whole saved group. It verifies the value before selecting the group's IDs **and again under the exclusive identity lock**, so another tab cannot change the group between selection and final player assignment. Individual manual ID assignment behavior is unchanged.
- `rust/volley-app/src/reid_native.rs` refuses a stale group document, preventing the ReID preview from unknowingly preserving manual groups from the wrong inference. Development compilation/formatting and build checks only; the final real-match E2E suite and Python-vs-Rust comparison remain deferred under the owner plan.
- Remaining: Rust-native image embedding extraction for uncached photos, global player registry editor, personal statistics/recounts, UI for manual group editing, and full GPU/CPU/WASM migration.

## 18. Native CPU player touch/action statistics — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL CPU + identity rewrite.** This endpoint reinterprets a **specific stored CPU result**, without rerunning GPU/CPU model inference. It does not provide complete original report/UI parity.

- `rust/volley-core/src/statistics.rs`: deterministic native action classification and de-duplication, mirroring `report_statistics.summarize_actions`. Seven contact classes are recognized in the timeline (attack, block, serve, dig, overhead_pass, low_touch, unknown_touch). Duplicates are collapsed by source timestamp (milliseconds) plus confirmed athlete identity, otherwise by unassigned Track ID or contact ID. Recognized technique outranks unknown_touch even if the latter has greater raw confidence; then confidence breaks ties. Counts include total touches, recognized touches, unassigned, per-action totals and per-Track ID audit rows.
- `rust/volley-app/src/player_statistics.rs`: `POST /api/players/statistics/native` with `mode:preview|recalculate`, `project_id`, explicit `run_id` and optional `selected_videos`. Verifies project, run, CPU cache ownership and video source before opening recorded `analysis/events.json` and optional classified `analysis/game-tracks.json`. Uses native rally reconstruction to accept only actions in classified rally windows when available. On legacy CPU runs without those tracks, shows a fallback count derived directly from saved actions, distinguished by null rally diagnostic.
- Only reviewed project-scoped `player_identities.json` assignments are counted as known players. Explicitly manual ReID candidate groups may propagate one already reviewed identity if all members can be physically distinct and no conflicting identity exists. Automatic ReID and geometric continuity never become real player assignments. Excluded IDs are removed. CPU replay's `players.replay.json` restricts visible Track ID actions when present.
- Aggregates are calculated **per video first**, then per athlete across selected videos, avoiding accidental merging of identical numeric Track IDs across different videos. The project-wide summary reports action totals, touches, activity relative to project median, and video-level diagnostics for filtered events, confirmed links, inherited manual links, conflicts and excluded IDs.
- `preview` is read-only. `recalculate` writes only with `VOLLEY_RUST_DATA_ROOT` set to an isolated root and stores versioned reports at `projects/<id>/reports/native-player-statistics/<run_id>-<input_sha24>.json`. An input SHA-256 includes algorithm version, run selection, roster, manual assignments, ReID groups and specific CPU event/track files; every file is checked for changes during processing. Identity reassignment uses the same exclusive per-project lock, and the source snapshots are immutable. The operation does not alter original CPU results or photo files.
- Outstanding: full native CPU event/touch classifier, rally correctness and inference parity, native ReID feature extraction, browser Statistics UI, per-action time clips for player-specific montage, global player editor, gold preset integration. The user-directed full match/E2E and Python-vs-Rust comparisons remain deferred until the entire rewrite is implemented.

## 19. Native personalized full-rally montage selection — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL end-to-end backend; no new browser controls or complete native CPU inference.** Works only from existing saved CPU and human-reviewed player identity evidence.

- `rust/volley-app/src/player_montage.rs`: new verified-athlete scoring stage for the *existing* `POST /api/montage/native/plan` and `POST /api/montage/native/start-from-run`. Both now accept optional `player_id` (existing project roster ID), `player_action` (all, attack, block, serve, dig, overhead_pass, low_touch, unknown_touch), and `min_player_actions` (1–100, default 1). These are rejected without a player ID if action filtering is requested. No arbitrary person name or guessed ReID association is accepted as a filter.
- The planner opens the exact saved GPU inference and selected CPU processing version for each video. Only manually reviewed Track ID assignments in the project's `player_identities.json`, plus conflict-free, physically non-overlapping *manual* ReID groups connected to one confirmed athlete, are considered identity. The model's automatic ReID proposals or geometric continuity are NEVER treated as confirmed athlete IDs. Excluded IDs and IDs filtered out by the chosen CPU `players.replay.json` are suppressed.
- CPU `analysis/events.json` is parsed once per video with the same timeline time/identity/dedup convention as native statistics: sort action candidates by CPU frame, use the accepted rally's **detected** start/end rather than padded clip boundaries, include only a matching verified pre-rally serve anchor. Duplicate action detections collapse by confirmed player/source millisecond with recognized technique preferred over unknown_touch. Reconstructed rally selection ID and source start/end frames stay unchanged; source-resolution clips remain complete.
- `player_action_counts`, `player_actions_total`, and `player_focus_matches` are exposed on candidate/selected scenes when athlete focus is enabled. `player_filter` includes project athlete ID/name, action and threshold, with `matching_player_rallies` count. Automatic ranking prioritizes the chosen athlete's own actions (or duration when explicitly requested), not other players' action totals. The ordinary whole-rally duration/confidence/ranking and max-duration gates still apply, and a manually chosen rally must be in the current focused candidate set or it is rejected as stale/out-of-scope.
- Existing no-athlete montage requests keep their prior request defaults and selection behavior. `start-from-run` reuses the same planner; it returns the selected complete-rally count and player focus metadata, rather than taking caller-supplied clip frames. No Python subprocess or new GPU pass is launched.
- The read path detects changing GPU source metadata, identity revision, ReID group revision, roster registry revision, saved CPU event revision and replay track metadata while building a plan. The original Python service, projects, GPU detections and reference photos are untouched.
- Remaining: player-specific montage UI, saved event-index optimization, true native event inference and precision checks, golden rally context-menu marking/filter, quick reel, vertical follow-ball overlays, complete Rust/WASM UI, packaging and full tests/adaptation/parity after rewrite completion.

## 20. Native gold-marked rallies and montage filters — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL BACKEND:** the Rust/WASM preview context menu and gold card CSS are still not built. This stage provides the verified stored-state/API contract only.

- `rust/volley-app/src/rally_marks.rs` introduces `POST /api/rallies/native/marks`. `action:read` returns the project/run-scoped gold bookmark registry and its SHA256 `revision` (or `absent` if the registry does not yet exist). `action:set` requires a particular `video`, `rally_id`, `marked:true/false`, and matching `expected_revision`. Explicit set is idempotent; unmarking removes only the specified bookmark. No arbitrary user-supplied source offsets or guessed rally IDs are accepted.
- The setter validates project, exact saved CPU run/video ownership, and regenerates the complete rally catalogue from immutable `game-tracks.json`/CPU events. The request may include the same `context_sec`, `gap_sec` and `rally_min_sec` used by the preview in order to validate the clicked `rally_id`. The saved `anchor_id` is a separate stable SHA24 of video, exact CPU cache version, **detected** play window and sorted ball Track IDs. Unlike the original `rally_id`, this anchor deliberately excludes context-padded source frame ranges, so an existing gold mark survives harmless padding/context adjustments but not a different source, CPU revision or resegmentation.
- Writes are confined to `<isolated VOLLEY_RUST_DATA_ROOT>/projects/<project_id>/rally_marks.json`. One bounded document (max 2 MiB/5,000 bookmarks), optimistic SHA revision, cross-process `.rally_marks.lock`, canonical-root and symlink checks, and atomic JSON replacement defend against accidental overwrites in multiple tabs. There are no writes to the Python production project, GPU inference or CPU result directory. Read-only inspection remains available without the isolated root.
- Both existing `POST /api/montage/native/plan` and `POST /api/montage/native/start-from-run` now accept `gold_filter: all|marked|unmarked` (default all). Each scene includes `gold_marked: bool`; the planner also returns `gold_marked_count` over reconstructed selected-run videos and its applied `gold_filter`. Gold filtering composes with verified-player action filtering, manual selected_rally_ids, and automatic whole-rally duration/quality gates. An **explicitly marked** unranked rally can be selected for an automatic gold-only montage if it passes its other minimum constraints. The renderer still uses the complete original-resolution source rally, not the touch segment or a padded card thumbnail.
- Unit fixtures have been written for anchor stability across different padding, separation of different CPU versions/windows, and optimistic revision changes. They remain unexecuted until the owner-requested final complete rewrite/test phase. Current compilation/formatting checks are incremental development diagnostics only.
- Remaining: native WASM card context menu on right-click ("Отметить" / "Снять отметку"), gold card styling, UI gold filter controls, quick reel, GPU/CPU rewrite completion, packaging, final real-match test/parity/regression stage.

## 21. Rust Quick Reel independent selection, preparation and job state — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL, NOT A COMPLETED QUICK REEL.** The native GPU and CPU analysis engines are not available yet; never claim this produces an MP4 or reuse previous inference results as a shortcut. This stage only completes the first production-style pipeline step and establishes the later engine contracts.

- `rust/volley-app/src/quick_reel.rs`: `POST /api/quick-reel/native/select` registers 1–30 explicitly selected absolute local video paths, validates extension and FFprobe video readability, records canonical source size/nanosecond mtime and returns 32-hex ephemeral tokens. Video files are **linked by path**, not copied into project storage. No older videos are suggested. A future native desktop file picker must supply the actual selected file paths; the legacy browser UI is not wired to this route.
- `POST /api/quick-reel/native/start` accepts the selection IDs and `audio_file` returned by the existing `POST /api/montage/native/audio` upload route. Verifies the same source identities again, the uploaded music container has an audio stream and lasts 10–600 seconds, then writes an independent request snapshot with current default GPU intent: VballNetV4c ball, RAVEL-013 players, beach mode, ONNX-CUDA, unlimited GPU memory, initial 0–15% preparation, 15–85% *fresh* GPU/CPU analysis, 85–100% original-source/ball-follow montage. No project profile or project result is linked. Each Quick Reel receives a unique cache salt; completed model detections must never be reused from an earlier run. CPU golden settings are explicitly **pending native port**, not falsely applied.
- `rust/volley-app/src/preparation.rs`: `prepare_standalone` reuses the exact same native FFmpeg/H.264/30-FPS content-addressed cache, lock and one-at-a-time preparation semaphore as native project preparation, without creating or touching an ordinary project. Analysis-ready source MP4s are used directly and already prepared compatible sources are reused. Otherwise FFmpeg is cancellable, source identity is checked after encoding and progress is persisted.
- The independent scratch root is `<VOLLEY_RUST_DATA_ROOT>/quick_reels/<uuid32>/`; only the latest request/queued/running status is retained. Source selection tokens reside in `quick_reels/selections`; shared preparation remains under `<isolated-root>/prepared` and is never deleted by scratch pruning. Repeated starts are serialized by an in-process gate and a cross-process `.active.lock`. Explicit cancel sends a watch signal and kills/awaits any active FFmpeg child, without interfering with an ordinary project. `GET /api/quick-reel/native/status` recovers the latest state after app restart and labels orphan queued/running statuses as interrupted.
- Once preparation succeeds, the persisted state is **awaiting_native_inference** at 15%. `native_inference_available:false`, `output:null` and a readable error prevent displaying a false ready MP4. Later GPU/CPU + rendering stages must be implemented as native Rust actions; the original Python quick-reel runner is never started.
- Safety: writes are disabled without a separate writable `VOLLEY_RUST_DATA_ROOT` outside the Python repository; writable quick/prepared directories are resolved and checked against symlinks, source files are only read, the user's actual saved Python projects and analyses are unchanged. No end-to-end parity tests or real match GPU benchmarks were run; the full owner-requested testing phase is deferred until the entire rewrite is feature-complete.
- Remaining: actual natively scheduled GPU RAVEL/Vball inference, full CPU touch and rally postprocess, native follow-ball video crop, MP4/audio render, completed-reel archive/history/download, trusted OS file picker + Rust/WASM frontend, and final quality/performance comparison with the original app.

## 22. Native ONNX Runtime one-clip GPU inference kernel — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL GPU R03.** The complete multi-video, persistent CPU/GPU analysis is **not** implemented. This milestone adds an actually callable native ONNX Runtime session/kernel and a read-only 9-frame diagnostic; no Python subprocess, project mutation or fabricated model outputs.

- New crate `rust/volley-inference/` depends on ONNX Runtime via `ort = 2.0.0-rc.10` with `load-dynamic` and `cuda` features, **not** a bundled GPU binary. Deployment must explicitly supply an ABI-matching ONNX Runtime 1.22 shared library, with CUDA/CuDNN dependencies, using `ORT_DYLIB_PATH`. Existing Python packages are not a trusted substitute. Compile checks alone do not prove that the runtime or CUDA provider is available.
- `rust/volley-inference/src/lib.rs`: strict fixed-sidecar specs for the repository's VballNetV4c and RAVEL-VB-013-9f ONNX releases; expected inputs `clip:[1,9,288,512]` float grayscale and `frames:[1,9,3,360,640]` float RGB; VballNet output `heatmaps:[1,18,288,512]` with nine radius maps; RAVEL outputs including `logits`, `boxes`, `temporal_association`, `ball_grid/size`, `action_logits/actor_logits`. Verifies file ownership, sidecar metadata, runtime input/output names and bounded tensor shapes. ONNX model inference runs through native `ort::Session`, not Python.
- `CUDAExecutionProvider` is requested explicitly; it must be advertised and its registration must succeed (`error_on_failure`), otherwise the call fails. It does **not** silently use a CPU-only session. The selected GPU device and optional MiB memory arena limit are enforced. `device:cpu` is reserved for explicit development diagnostics and is never an automatic fallback.
- `rust/volley-app/src/inference_native.rs`: read-only `POST /api/inference/native/preflight` checks the two fixed model sidecars and (only if `probe_runtime:true`) tries opening real ORT sessions. It reports runtime session errors explicitly without launching a long-running inference or modifying model output caches.
- `rust/volley-app/src/inference_clip.rs`: read-only `POST /api/inference/native/clip-preview` accepts an existing project/video reference, fixed model kind, starting frame, CUDA device and CPU threads. Resolves the project's validated **prepared** video, decodes exactly nine 30-FPS frames via FFmpeg into bounded raw RGB/gray pixels, normalizes to `[0,1]` and the model's required layout, then calls native `Engine::infer_clip`. Returns raw VballNet confidence peaks/radius fractions or RAVEL per-frame highest Touch candidate and each named output tensor shape; neither is a confirmed ball/contact or identified player. Data remain in memory and are never written into the immutable original GPU/CPU inference cache.
- FFmpeg uses bilinear scaling; exact OpenCV pixel parity, original RAVEL temporal stride, model output postprocessing, per-frame stable player tracking, full-ball trajectory and model cache publication are still pending. The code has only passed `cargo fmt/check/build`, NOT actual ONNX session execution on this WSL host or real-match quality tests. Full GPU/CPU integration and final Python-vs-Rust comparison must precede switching the production app.
- Quick Reel intentionally remains at `awaiting_native_inference`: a single-clip diagnostic is NOT a substitute for processing every frame and generating a complete verified CPU rally analysis.

## 23. Native global player catalog editor — 2026-11-08

Mutations require `expected_revision` (`absent` or 64-hex SHA256); revision is checked under an exclusive cross-process lock. Registry JSON is written atomically; reject corrupt/oversized files, symlinked target or directory and invalid lock symlinks. No implicit delete of players/photos. Existing `POST /api/project-update` remains the native project roster editor; direct attach/detach convenience actions and whole UI are still pending.

## 26. Bounded raw-frame streaming windows and shared ONNX input layout — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL R03 decoder groundwork; not a complete GPU run.** Added `rust/volley-media/src/frame_windows.rs` with `FrameWindows<R:Read>`: a bounded nine-frame sliding ring buffer from a contiguous raw-video stream, configurable temporal stride 1–9, exact frame offsets, explicit truncated-frame errors, strict frame-byte and maximum-video bounds, and no silent short-tail padding. A future single FFmpeg process per prepared source can feed this directly rather than seeking the source for every GPU window. The stream reader itself is pure Rust and does not open an FFmpeg child or run any model yet.

- `normalized_model_window` is the shared exact `[0,1]` input conversion for 9-frame grayscale `[T,H,W]` or RGB `[T,C,H,W]`; the existing RAVEL/VballNet clip diagnostic now calls this contract instead of duplicating its conversion loop. FFmpeg interpolation parity and RAVEL temporal stride/overlap policy remain unverified; no touch or ball output is labelled confirmed.

## 27. Single-FFmpeg raw-frame window streaming — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL R03, no full GPU analysis:** Added `rust/volley-media/src/raw_decoder.rs` and exported it from `volley-media/src/lib.rs`. The native decoder starts one bounded FFmpeg subprocess for a validated prepared-video path, streams RGB/gray raw pixels directly into `FrameWindows`, and invokes a callback for each complete nine-frame window. Stride 1–9, at most 120 windows per invocation, explicit frame offset, 48-hour upper bound, no unbounded video buffering, no invented/padded tail frames. FFmpeg is killed/reaped on callback error, decoder failure or cancellation; bounded stderr tail is drained concurrently to avoid deadlocks. Output carries a window/decoded-frame count, not a claimed video inference result.

- Development verification: WSL `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo check --workspace --offline`, `cargo test -p volley-media --offline raw_decoder` (2 passed, including a real synthetic FFmpeg MP4 decode). FFmpeg time/frame-content parity, CUDA provider runtime, full-video immutable cache publication and advanced cancellation under pathological blocked decoders remain outstanding. This decoder is only the input stage for later persistent ONNX processing; Python remains production.

## 28. Persistent ONNX session over bounded native FFmpeg stream — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL R03 diagnostic only.** Added `rust/volley-app/src/inference_stream.rs` and the read-only route `POST /api/inference/native/window-scan`. Select a known project, one member video, model kind `ball|players`, `first_frame`, 1–120 nine-frame windows (default 8), temporal `stride` 1–9 (default 9), explicit CUDA/CPU provider parameters. The request validates project/video membership, the original source's prepared-file ownership, window end relative to FFprobe duration and 48-hour bound, and source size/mtime before and after scanning. One real ONNX session is opened once and reused for every window while one FFmpeg stream emits bounded frames. CUDA provider failure remains explicit; CPU is only an explicitly requested diagnostic mode. No Python workers are invoked.

- Returns one result per window containing the original raw VballNet heatmap peak/radius or RAVEL Touch proposal peak, together with named tensor shapes, decoded-frame count and stride. Overlapped temporal windows remain separate raw model proposals, not confirmed contacts or player identities. The route cannot advertise completion of GPU inference and writes no cache or project result. It shares the inference semaphore with single-window preflight/preview and rejects attempts to scan beyond an input video's known bounds.
- Development checks: WSL `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo check --workspace --offline`, and `cargo test -p volley-app --offline inference_stream` (2 tests passed). Actual full CUDA/ORT runtime checks, complete model output decoding/tracking, durable inference snapshots, handling of long videos, cooperative cancel and final parity benchmarks are still outstanding. Do not switch production or claim ready Quick Reel.

## 30. Touch smoothing regression fixture corrected; full Rust unit suite green — 2026-11-08

This contradicts the reference Python NumPy moving average (`0.95/5 = 0.19`), which legitimately suppresses the isolated peak at 0.20. The test case in `rust/volley-core/src/touch.rs` now uses threshold 0.18 when expecting both events, and a separate regression test verifies that the original threshold 0.20 suppresses the isolated event. Production neural probabilities, peak selection, contact gating and Track ID attribution code are unchanged.

## 31. Native RAVEL-013/VballNet ONNX detection decoder — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL R03/R04.** Added `rust/volley-inference/src/decode.rs` with a provider-independent, strictly shape-checked conversion of native ONNX tensors to *unconfirmed, unlinked* frame detections (`RawDetection`): RAVEL logits `[1,9,Q,2]`, normalized cxcywh boxes, per-query Touch log-probabilities, head-point estimates, `ball_grid` offset/score and `ball_size` dimensions; VballNetV4c heatmap peak and radius channels. Uses a conservative default raw player confidence floor 0.05 and retains up to 32 strongest player proposals per frame **without NMS** so future CPU postprocessing can change thresholds without an extra GPU run. Invalid tensor layouts and non-finite/invalid boxes are rejected or ignored. No raw query ID is promoted to a stable global Track ID; no neural action is deemed a verified contact.

- `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo test -p volley-inference --offline` (5 tests passed including player/ball/Touch synthetic tensor fixtures), and `cargo check --workspace --offline` passed in WSL. Decoder output is not yet wired to whole-video GPU worker or canonical inference files; true end-to-end ONNX/CUDA parity and quantitative performance/accuracy remain to be verified after rewrite completion.

## 32. Full prepared-video FFmpeg streaming input for native ONNX — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL R03.** `rust/volley-media/src/raw_decoder.rs` now supports `decode_full_video`: one FFmpeg process streams every decoded 30-fps prepared-video frame to a bounded nine-frame sliding window, producing correctly indexed windows at stride 1..9 until EOF. It neither seeks between windows nor buffers the whole recording. End-of-video fragments smaller than nine frames are **not padded or presented as inferred**; absence of a complete nine-frame window is an error. FFmpeg exit status is verified before results may be committed. Callback errors and cooperative per-window cancellation terminate/reap the child; stderr remains bounded and concurrently drained.

- WSL `cargo test -p volley-media --offline raw_decoder` (3 tests) and `cargo check --workspace --offline` passed; a real synthetic 18-frame MPEG4 video produced starts 0,4,8 at stride4, retained the full decoded frame count, and cancellation was verified. **Not yet a whole-GPU-run feature:** the ONNX app still needs to invoke this stream and publish complete detection data, with progress, cancellation, and actual model provider tests. Blocking FFmpeg reads cannot yet be interrupted mid-read; production must not switch.

RAVEL raw detections include player rectangles, unconfirmed per-query Touch probabilities, head points and ball-grid candidates. VballNet raw detections include the ball box derived from peak/radius maps. Query indices are window-local hints only; no Track ID or contact identity is confirmed. The existing isolated immutable evidence route can store these richer proposals, subject to its explicit 2-MiB JSON size cap.

## 34. Native full-video model detection job lifecycle — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL R03; not yet a complete Studio inference run.** Added `rust/volley-app/src/inference_full.rs`, a single-model, whole-prepared-video ONNX worker. New APIs: `POST /api/inference/native/full/start` (`project_id`, `video`, `model:ball|players`, optional `stride:1..9` default 9, explicit `device` and resource settings), `GET /api/inference/native/full/status?project_id=...&job_id=...`, and `POST /api/inference/native/full/cancel`. The job loads one persistent ONNX Runtime model session, reads a single bounded FFmpeg raw stream for the whole video, converts model outputs into per-frame raw detections and appends JSONL instead of accumulating an entire match in RAM. Each job receives an immutable 32-hex ID in the isolated project directory `<VOLLEY_RUST_DATA_ROOT>/projects/<project>/native_gpu_runs/<job_id>/`, with durable `status.json`. On successful completion, `detections.jsonl.partial` is flushed/synced/renamed, and `manifest.json` records source size/mtime, model/device, 30-fps stride, frames/windows and SHA-256 of the recorded JSONL. Explicitly marks **raw candidates only; no stable Track IDs, no confirmed Touch events, no completed overall GPU/CPU studio analysis**. The next worker can process the other model separately; canonical combined inference and CPU snapshot publication are pending.

- Writes require an isolated Rust root, exact project/video ownership, validated 30-fps prepared source, safe project/job directories, and per-project archive locks during start. One active full-model job per Rust process; the inference semaphore is shared with existing clip scans. Progress is periodically persisted. Cancellation is cooperative between windows (no forced interruption of a blocked native ONNX operation yet), and archiving a project cancels its active model worker before moving the folder. Restarted incomplete jobs are reported as interrupted, not complete. A missing ORT runtime/model creates an explicit failed state with no successful manifest. Existing Python service, source media, common preparation cache, player photos and historical analysis are never modified.
- WSL `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo check --workspace --offline` and `cargo test -p volley-app --offline inference_full` (3 tests) passed, including a synthetic MP4 project fixture with deliberately missing model files: missing model cannot generate a fake successful GPU result, original recording remains unchanged, and no result manifest is published. CUDA runtime, full real-model video and eventual integration parity have **not** been verified. Existing ONNX Runtime shared libraries on this machine are versions 1.29/1.31; the Rust ORT crate currently targets the 1.22 API and runtime ABI must be checked before a GPU smoke run. Production switch is still prohibited.

For CUDA it assembles `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` from the sibling native Nvidia CUDA/CuDNN library directories, without invoking Python for inference. The script itself uses Python solely to parse JSON smoke-test responses; the application and ONNX forward path remain Rust/native. Proper cleanup restores the read-only development test service.

CUDA test: VballNet one tensor / eight raw candidate balls; RAVEL 11 output tensors / no player candidates from all-zero inputs (not an accuracy score). No Python inference subprocess and no silent CPU fallback.

Both completed on CUDA with two windows of nine frames, raw JSONL manifests saved, output SHA-256 verified, original test media unchanged, no fabricated combined GPU/CPU project run. VballNet JSONL was 3038 bytes and RAVEL JSONL 3112 bytes for this synthetic fixture. Thus the **single-model raw-detection whole-video worker works on real CUDA**; multi-hour footage, original match output fidelity and combined CPU/GPU acceptance remain untested. A CUDA preflight initially failed because `libcublasLt.so.13` was not on the dynamic loader path; adding the existing `nvidia/cu13/lib` and `nvidia/cudnn/lib` directories resolved it without installing or modifying CUDA packages.
- To re-run explicitly from WSL: `bash scripts/ci/smoke_rust_ort.sh /absolute/path/to/libonnxruntime.so cpu full` or replace `cpu` with `cuda`. The ignored tests are not part of regular `cargo test`. No production service restart, user-data mutation or change to `master` occurred.

**PARTIAL R03/R04, not yet the complete CPU postprocessor.** Added `rust/volley-core/src/fusion.rs` and `rust/volley-app/src/inference_fusion.rs` with `POST /api/inference/native/fuse` (`project_id`, `video`, `ravel_job_id`, `vball_job_id`), plus `GET /api/inference/native/fusion?project_id=...&fusion_id=...` to inspect an immutable fusion manifest. Source media, historical Python cache and original GPU evidence remain unchanged; invalid/conflicting sources fail without a published fusion manifest.

## 38. Provisional CPU Track ID reconstruction from RAVEL boxes — 2026-11-08

**PARTIAL R04 — pure core primitive, not yet wired to fused GPU job input.** Added `rust/volley-core/src/tracker.rs` and registered it in `rust/volley-core/src/lib.rs`. The deterministic bounded-memory `ProvisionalTracker` turns sequential RAVEL per-frame player boxes into fresh tracklet IDs from 1, without interpreting RAVEL local `query_index` as a cross-window identity. Configurable CPU-only defaults: player minimum confidence 0.30, near-identical proposal NMS IoU 0.88, max automatically bridged absence 12 frames at 30 fps, minimum association score 0.48, reciprocal ambiguity margin 0.11. Matching combines IoU, predicted center distance, and size consistency; simultaneous assignments are strictly one-to-one. Ambiguous crossings produce new tracklets instead of assigning different players the same ID. Touch log-probabilities and head-point evidence are preserved in original candidate records; no identity is assigned to an athlete.

- Gaps longer than 12 frames **do not** automatically rejoin. The earlier 4-second geometry-based continuity module remains a manual *suggestion* process only, since geometry alone cannot prove two appearances show one athlete. No forced interpolation or fabricated player detections.
- WSL `cargo fmt --all`, `cargo test -p volley-core --offline tracker` (6 tests) and `cargo check --workspace --offline` passed. Tests cover query-index permutation, duplicates versus concurrent detections, ambiguous crossings, short occlusion, long separation, invalid options/non-finite inputs. Full replay integration and final precision/recall comparisons remain pending.

Each `tracked_frames.jsonl` row contains the selected ball, raw candidates and tracked-player records with `is_verified_athlete:false`; it does **not** assign a person from the roster or claim any confirmed Touch.
